  94. The correct answer is B (Aggregated statistics on the number, type, and root causes of breaches over the reporting period.): An annual breach trend report intended to inform leadership decision-making is most useful when it presents aggregated, de-identified statistics on the number and type of breaches, common root causes, and the corrective actions taken or planned, allowing leadership to identify systemic risks and allocate resources for improvement. Including unredacted clinical notes or naming specific patients would itself risk creating a new privacy breach through the report itself. Irrelevant personal information about employees, such as home addresses, has no place in this type of summary report. Well-structured trend reporting supports continuous improvement and demonstrates organizational accountability to both leadership and, where applicable, oversight bodies.
  95. The correct answer is B (Consider alternative or substituted forms of notification, such as a secure patient portal message or another reliable indirect method.): When reasonable direct contact attempts by phone or mail are unsuccessful, most breach notification frameworks expect the custodian to consider alternative or substituted methods of notification reasonably likely to reach the patient, such as a secure patient portal message, while thoroughly documenting every attempt made to demonstrate good-faith compliance efforts. Simply abandoning the effort without documentation would fail to meet the custodian's notification obligation. Notifying an unrelated or unauthorized third party, such as extended family without proper authority, would itself risk creating an additional privacy breach. Publishing identifiable patient information publicly would be a serious violation of the very privacy interests notification is meant to protect, rather than an appropriate substitute method.
  96. The correct answer is B (Retention periods vary by province and by the type of record, governed by applicable provincial legislation.): Retention obligations for health records in Canada are not governed by one uniform national rule but instead vary depending on the province, the type of record involved, such as adult versus pediatric records, and applicable regulatory college or institutional policy requirements. A health information management professional must identify and apply the specific retention schedule relevant to the jurisdiction and record type in question rather than assuming a single universal period applies everywhere. This variability is an important reason why organizations maintain detailed, jurisdiction-specific retention schedules as part of their records management program. Relying on a remembered fixed number from a different context risks non-compliance.
  97. The correct answer is A (Pediatric records are generally retained for a period tied to the age of majority.): Many Canadian provincial retention frameworks recognize that a minor could not exercise certain legal rights, such as bringing a claim, until reaching the age of majority, and therefore often extend the retention clock for pediatric records to run from the date the individual reaches adulthood rather than simply from the date of last treatment. This commonly results in pediatric records being retained for a comparatively longer period than an equivalent adult record. This is a general principle reflected in various forms across provinces rather than a single specific universal number. HIM retention schedules typically address pediatric records as a distinct category for this reason.
  98. The correct answer is A (Cross-cut shredding or an equivalent secure destruction method that renders the information unreadable and unreconstructable.): Secure destruction of health records generally requires a method, such as cross-cut shredding, pulping, or incineration through a certified destruction service, that renders the information permanently unreadable and unreconstructable, protecting patient privacy even after the record's useful life has ended. Simply placing intact records in an unlocked recycling bin or leaving them unattended would expose sensitive information to unauthorized viewing and would not meet any recognized secure destruction standard. Donating intact records containing identifiable patient information would constitute an unauthorized disclosure regardless of the recipient's intended use. Custodians typically document destruction, including a certificate of destruction when using a third-party vendor, to demonstrate compliance.
  99. The correct answer is C (Arrangements must be made for the continued safe custody, retention, and eventual destruction of the records.): When a facility closes, the custodian remains responsible for ensuring continued compliance with retention obligations for existing patient records, which typically requires making arrangements for safe custody, ongoing access for continuity of care, and eventual proper disposal once retention periods expire, often through a formal transfer of custody to another custodian, a regional health authority, or a designated records repository. Simply discarding records because a facility is closing would violate retention obligations and could seriously disrupt continuity of patient care. Patients are not generally required to personally retrieve original charts, since the record itself, not just the patient's own copy, must remain properly retained and accessible under an appropriate custodian. Advance planning for facility closures is an important, often overlooked HIM responsibility.
  100. The correct answer is B (A patient's right to access a record generally exists only while the custodian still retains that record.): A patient's statutory right to access their own health information is inherently tied to the continued existence of that record, meaning the right can practically be exercised only while the custodian is still retaining the record, whether because the retention period has not yet expired or because the custodian has chosen to retain it longer. Once a record has been lawfully and securely destroyed after its retention period expires, there is no longer an existing record for the patient to access, though the custodian should be able to confirm what retention schedule applied. This relationship underscores why proper retention scheduling is important not just for compliance but also for supporting patients' ongoing access rights during the retention period. A change of address has no bearing on the existence or accessibility of the underlying record.
  101. The correct answer is B (Retention scheduling should apply consistently to the authoritative record.): Where an original record has been digitized, the organization must determine, based on applicable legal and regulatory rules along with its own documented policy, which version constitutes the authoritative record going forward for retention purposes, and apply the relevant retention schedule to that version, since retention obligations attach to maintaining the required health information rather than to any single specific physical format. Digitization does not eliminate the underlying retention obligation for the information itself, even if the original physical medium is eventually destroyed once a compliant digital copy exists. Assuming the original film must be retained forever regardless of any applicable framework would not reflect the general, format-flexible approach most provincial rules take to satisfying retention requirements. Clear documented policy on this issue is an important part of a defensible records management program.
  102. The correct answer is C (Place a litigation hold on the record, suspending its scheduled destruction until the legal matter is resolved.): When a record eligible for scheduled destruction is or may reasonably become relevant to active or anticipated litigation, standard records management practice calls for placing a litigation hold on that specific record, suspending its destruction until legal counsel confirms the hold can be lifted, in order to preserve evidence and avoid the serious legal consequences that can result from destroying potentially relevant records. This obligation generally overrides the routine retention schedule for the specific affected record, even though the schedule still applies to other, unrelated records. Transferring a record directly to an opposing party would be entirely inappropriate and is not how litigation-related disclosure is properly handled. HIM professionals often play an important role in identifying and flagging records subject to a litigation hold, working closely with legal counsel.
  103. The correct answer is D (Because retention periods, exceptions, and record type classifications can differ meaningfully between provinces.): Because Canadian health record retention obligations arise primarily from provincial legislation, regulations, and applicable professional college requirements rather than a single uniform national standard, the specific periods, record type classifications, and exceptions can differ meaningfully from one province to another. Simply copying a retention rule from a different province's framework without independently verifying it against the rules actually governing the organization's own jurisdiction risks either under-retaining records the organization was legally required to keep longer, or over-retaining records unnecessarily. This is why HIM professionals must maintain retention schedules specifically tailored to and verified against their own applicable provincial and institutional requirements. Assuming complete national uniformity, or that federal law fully displaces provincial retention rules, would be a significant and risky misunderstanding of the actual legal landscape.
  104. The correct answer is A (An ongoing clinical need, an active legal hold, or a specific, justified institutional policy.): A custodian may have legitimate, documented reasons to retain a specific record beyond the applicable statutory minimum, such as an ongoing clinical relationship where the information remains actively relevant to care, an active litigation hold, or a specific, justified institutional policy addressing a particular category of high-value clinical information. Retention decisions should still be principled and documented rather than driven by simple administrative convenience or an unexplained general reluctance to manage the destruction process, since indefinite retention without justification can itself create unnecessary privacy risk by keeping more identifiable information in existence than needed. Good HIM practice involves periodically reviewing whether continued retention beyond the minimum remains justified. This reflects the broader privacy principle that organizations should not retain personal information longer than necessary for identified purposes.
  105. The correct answer is C (Immediately correct the retention schedule going forward for remaining pediatric records, document the discrepancy.): Upon discovering that pediatric records were destroyed under an incorrect, too-short retention timeline, the appropriate response is to immediately correct the retention schedule going forward to properly reflect the extended period applicable to minors, thoroughly document the discrepancy that was found, and assess whether the improper prior destruction constitutes an incident requiring further action, potentially including notification to an applicable oversight body depending on the jurisdiction and circumstances. While records already improperly destroyed generally cannot be recreated, addressing the systemic cause prevents the same error from continuing to affect other pediatric records. Simply continuing the flawed practice or destroying unrelated adult records early would not remedy the underlying compliance failure. This scenario illustrates why regular, thorough retention schedule audits are an important quality assurance function within health information management.
  106. The correct answer is C (The hospital generally remains legally responsible as the custodian for ensuring the records are retained, secured.): Transferring physical custody of records to a third-party storage vendor generally does not eliminate the hospital's own underlying legal responsibility as the health information custodian for ensuring those records continue to be properly retained, secured, and eventually disposed of in compliance with applicable requirements, since custodianship is typically defined by legal accountability rather than mere physical possession. The hospital is expected to have a formal agreement with the vendor addressing security safeguards, confidentiality, retention compliance, and secure destruction procedures, and to exercise appropriate oversight of the vendor's practices. Assuming responsibility simply disappears once records physically leave the premises would be inconsistent with how custodianship obligations generally work under provincial health privacy law. This is a key reason vendor management and contractual safeguards are an important part of records management programs.
  107. The correct answer is C (To establish, for each category of record, how long it must be kept to satisfy legal and clinical requirements.): A records retention schedule is a foundational health information management tool that establishes, for each defined category of record, the minimum period it must be retained to satisfy applicable legal requirements along with genuine clinical and administrative needs, and the point at which it may be appropriately and securely destroyed consistent with privacy principles limiting how long personal information is kept. This structured approach helps an organization avoid both the compliance risk of destroying records too early and the privacy and storage risk of keeping records indefinitely without justification. It is not intended to create pointless bureaucracy but rather to provide clear, defensible guidance for day-to-day records management decisions. Retention schedules are typically reviewed and updated periodically as legislation or institutional needs change.
  108. The correct answer is A (Custodians are generally required to retain records for the legally mandated minimum period to meet clinical, legal, and administrative needs.): Health information custodians are generally required by applicable retention legislation and regulation to retain records for a legally mandated minimum period to support continuity of care, potential legal or regulatory needs, and public health or quality assurance purposes, meaning an individual patient's informal request for early destruction does not override this legal obligation. This reflects that health records serve interests beyond just the individual patient's own preference, including protecting the patient's own future care needs and the custodian's own accountability. Patients generally do have other rights, such as placing a lock box restriction on access or disclosure, that address privacy concerns without requiring premature destruction of the underlying record. Clinics should be prepared to explain this distinction clearly and respectfully when patients raise this kind of request.
  109. The correct answer is A (Using a certified data destruction method, such as physical destruction or cryptographic erasure verified to render the data permanently unrecoverable.): Secure disposal of electronic media that once stored personal health information requires a certified data destruction method, such as physical destruction of the drive or a verified cryptographic erasure or degaussing process, that renders the previously stored data genuinely unrecoverable, since a standard operating system delete function typically only removes file references rather than the underlying data itself. Donating or reselling a drive without proper wiping, or leaving it unsecured indefinitely, both create significant risk that residual patient data could be recovered by an unauthorized party. Many organizations use a certified third-party vendor and retain a certificate of destruction to document compliance with this requirement. This is an important and sometimes overlooked technical security dimension of records retention and disposal policy.
  110. The correct answer is C (Chain of custody and accurate tracking of exactly which records moved where must be maintained throughout.): During a large-scale consolidation of archived records, maintaining a clear chain of custody, continued physical and administrative security safeguards throughout transport and storage, and accurate, verifiable tracking of exactly which records moved where and when is essential to prevent records from being lost, damaged, or exposed to unauthorized access during the transition. Treating the move as a purely logistical exercise where speed overrides privacy and security considerations, or leaving records temporarily unsecured, would create significant compliance and patient care risks. All records involved in the consolidation, not merely the most recently created ones, need to be properly tracked to ensure nothing is misplaced or unaccounted for. Detailed planning and documentation for this kind of large consolidation project is a significant HIM responsibility.
  111. The correct answer is C (Some jurisdictions apply distinct, often longer, retention considerations to certain public health related records.): Because certain public health related records, such as those involving reportable communicable diseases, can carry ongoing epidemiological and surveillance value beyond an individual patient's own direct clinical care, some provincial frameworks and public health legislation apply distinct retention considerations to this category, which can differ from the general retention treatment of routine clinical records. The specific treatment still varies meaningfully by jurisdiction and record type, consistent with the broader principle that no single universal Canada-wide retention rule applies to all records. Extremely short, fixed destruction timelines like twenty-four hours are not a general feature of this framework and would be inconsistent with legitimate public health surveillance needs. HIM professionals working with public health data should be familiar with any additional, jurisdiction-specific rules that may apply to this category beyond the general clinical record retention schedule.
  112. The correct answer is A (These registers may have their own applicable retention requirements distinct from individual patient charts.): Aggregate procedural records such as operating room registers, while distinct from individual patient charts, often have their own applicable retention considerations because they support quality assurance review, accreditation processes, and can become relevant in legal proceedings even though they are organized differently than a single patient's record. A comprehensive HIM retention schedule should address this category specifically rather than assuming it carries no retention significance at all. Retention obligations apply to the health information itself regardless of whether it is stored electronically or on paper, and regardless of whether it takes the form of an individual chart or an aggregate register or log. Properly classifying and scheduling this kind of institutional record is part of a mature, comprehensive records management program.
  113. The correct answer is A (Explain that retention obligations generally continue to apply after a patient's death for the legally mandated retention period.): A patient's death does not generally eliminate the custodian's retention obligations for that patient's health record, and most provincial retention frameworks expect the record to continue to be retained for the applicable legally mandated period even after death, since the record can remain relevant to matters such as estate administration, potential legal claims, or public health purposes. The custodian should explain this to the estate representative rather than agreeing to immediate destruction simply because the patient has died, and should not condition compliance with the actual applicable rules on payment of a fee. Transferring the physical or electronic record itself to a family member or representative, rather than following a proper access or disclosure process, would also be inconsistent with how custodianship and confidentiality obligations continue to apply after death. HIM staff handling estate-related requests should be familiar with the specific rules on both continued retention and any distinct access rights that may apply to a deceased patient's record.
  114. The correct answer is A (The specific applicable provincial legislation, regulation, or professional college retention standard governing that record type.): The defensible, correct source for justifying a specific retention period is the actual applicable provincial legislation, regulation, or relevant professional regulatory college retention standard governing that particular record type within that specific jurisdiction, together with any institutional policy that has been properly built on top of, and consistent with, that underlying legal framework. Relying on a personal guess, an unrelated period recalled from a different country's system, or simple administrative convenience would not withstand scrutiny during an accreditation review and could reflect a genuine compliance gap if the schedule does not actually align with the applicable legal requirements. Because these specific periods vary by province and record type, as emphasized throughout Canadian health information management practice, professionals must be able to point to the correct, current, jurisdiction-specific source. Maintaining clear documentation linking each schedule entry to its legal basis is considered a best practice for exactly this kind of accountability.
  115. The correct answer is C (Establishing and following a retention schedule that ties each record category's retention period to identified legal, clinical, and administrative purposes.): The privacy principle limiting retention is best reflected by establishing a structured retention schedule that ties each category of record to the specific legal, clinical, and administrative purposes actually justifying its retention, and then following through with secure destruction once those purposes have been satisfied and any legally mandated minimum period has passed. This avoids both the privacy risk of indefinite, unjustified retention and the compliance risk of destroying records before legitimate purposes and legal minimums have been met. Destroying records immediately upon creation would fail to satisfy the clinical continuity and legal purposes that retention schedules exist to serve in the first place. This principle applies equally to records regardless of format, whether paper or electronic, since the underlying personal information and privacy interest are the same.
  116. The correct answer is C (Inpatient care, services requiring formal hospital admission and at least one overnight stay.): inpatient care refers to services provided to patients who are formally admitted to a hospital and occupy a bed, typically involving at least one overnight stay, as distinct from outpatient or ambulatory care where patients receive treatment and return home the same day. This distinction is fundamental to hospital case classification, coding, and funding methodologies used across the Canadian health system. HIM professionals must correctly distinguish inpatient from outpatient encounters for accurate data reporting.
  117. The correct answer is A (Ambulatory or outpatient care, services delivered without any overnight hospital stay.): ambulatory or outpatient care describes services delivered to patients who do not require an overnight hospital stay, including same-day surgeries, diagnostic testing, and outpatient clinic visits, allowing patients to return home the same day. This is distinct from inpatient care, which requires formal admission and at least one overnight stay. The growth of ambulatory care has been a significant trend in the Canadian health system as more procedures shift away from traditional inpatient stays.
  118. The correct answer is C (An aging population combined with efforts to reduce hospital costs and support independent living.): Canada's aging population, along with policy efforts to reduce costly hospital stays and support patient preferences to remain independent for as long as possible, has driven significant investment and expansion in home and community care services across provinces. This shift reflects broader system goals of providing the right care in the right setting at the right cost. Understanding this trend helps explain ongoing policy and funding discussions in Canadian health care.
  119. The correct answer is D (Community-based care, support delivered within a person's own community rather than a facility.): community-based care delivers health and social support services within the client's own community setting rather than in an institutional facility, allowing individuals to receive ongoing support, such as mental health counselling, while maintaining independent living. This model contrasts with acute inpatient psychiatric care, which is reserved for patients requiring intensive, hospital-based treatment. The shift toward community-based models has been a notable trend in Canadian mental health service delivery.
  120. The correct answer is A (A classification of care intensity from primary care through acute, rehabilitative, and long-term care.): levels of care describes the spectrum of intensity and setting across which health services are organized, from lower-intensity primary and preventive care through higher-intensity acute hospital treatment, and on to rehabilitative, long-term, and palliative care as a patient's needs evolve. This framework helps health system planners and HIM professionals understand how resources and patients move through the system. Correctly matching a scenario to its level of care is a recurring exam skill.
  121. The correct answer is D (Tertiary care involves highly specialized, often referral-based services requiring advanced expertise and technology not widely available.): tertiary care refers to highly specialized medical services, often requiring referral from primary or secondary care providers, delivered at select hospitals equipped with the advanced technology, subspecialist expertise, and infrastructure needed for complex procedures like organ transplantation or advanced cardiac surgery. General acute care, by contrast, covers a broader range of more common hospital services available at most community hospitals. This distinction affects how patients are referred and how services are regionally concentrated.
  122. The correct answer is B (Strong primary care manages routine needs, coordinating referrals and reducing pressure on acute care.): because primary care providers manage the majority of a population's ongoing and preventive health needs and serve as the entry point and coordinator for referrals to specialized services, a well-functioning primary care system can reduce avoidable emergency department visits and hospital admissions. This is why health system planning frequently prioritizes primary care investment as a foundation for overall system sustainability. This concept links directly to funding and continuum of care planning discussions.
  123. The correct answer is A (Long-term care is delivered in a licensed facility, while home care is delivered at home.): long-term care refers to ongoing supportive and nursing care delivered in a licensed residential facility for individuals who can no longer live independently, while home care delivers similar supportive, nursing, or rehabilitative services within a person's own home, often allowing them to remain in the community longer. Both serve individuals with ongoing care needs, but the setting and level of independence differ significantly. This distinction is important for correctly classifying care settings in health information systems.
  124. The correct answer is B (Preventive or public health screening, population level testing before symptoms ever appear.): newborn screening programs are population-level preventive health initiatives designed to detect conditions early, before symptoms develop, allowing for timely intervention, and are typically organized and overseen through provincial public health or newborn screening programs. This falls under the preventive and public health component of the continuum of care rather than acute treatment, which responds to an already-present illness or injury. Preventive screening programs are a core public health function tested within this domain.
  125. The correct answer is B (Incomplete information transfer can cause care gaps, medication errors, or preventable readmissions.): when a patient moves between care settings, such as from a hospital discharge to home care or long-term care, incomplete or delayed transfer of clinical information can result in medication errors, missed follow-up needs, or preventable hospital readmission, making accurate information flow essential to continuum of care effectiveness. Health information management professionals play a direct role in designing and maintaining systems that support this safe transfer, subject to applicable privacy legislation. This is a practical link between HIM practice and continuum of care quality.
  126. The correct answer is D (Coordinating services across the full continuum of care to meet population health needs efficiently.): integrated regional planning across primary, acute, long-term, and home care reflects the broader health system goal of matching resources to population needs across the full continuum of care, aiming to provide the right service in the right setting at the right time. Regional health authorities are often structured specifically to enable this kind of cross-continuum coordination within a defined geographic population. This integrated planning approach is a recurring theme in Canadian health system governance.
  127. The correct answer is C (Health Canada, the federal department that administers the Canada Health Act.): Health Canada is the federal department responsible for administering the Canada Health Act, regulating drugs and medical devices, and setting broad national health policy direction, including monitoring provincial and territorial compliance with the Act's five principles. This is distinct from the Public Health Agency of Canada, which focuses on public health surveillance and emergency preparedness as a separate federal agency. Knowing which body performs which function is a core recall competency for the health care system domain.
  128. The correct answer is B (The Public Health Agency of Canada, which leads national public health surveillance.): the Public Health Agency of Canada, established in 2004 following the SARS outbreak, is the federal agency responsible for public health surveillance, infectious disease control, emergency preparedness, and coordinating national responses to public health threats. It operates separately from Health Canada, which retains broader regulatory and policy functions. This distinction became especially prominent during national responses to pandemics and other public health emergencies.
  129. The correct answer is B (The Canadian Institute for Health Information, which standardizes and reports national health data.): CIHI is an independent, not-for-profit organization that collects, standardizes, and publicly reports health system and health workforce data from across Canada, including hospital discharge abstract data used for case-mix and funding purposes. CIHI does not deliver care or set health policy directly, but its data holdings and standards underpin much of the analytic and reporting work performed by health information management professionals. Its role is central to the CHIM curriculum given how closely it intersects with coding, classification, and funding data.
  130. The correct answer is D (Accreditation Canada, which conducts voluntary external reviews against national quality standards.): Accreditation Canada is a not-for-profit organization that conducts voluntary, standards-based external reviews of hospitals and other health organizations, assessing them against national quality and patient safety standards and granting accreditation status to organizations that meet those standards. This process is distinct from government regulation, since accreditation is generally voluntary though often expected or required by funders and provincial oversight bodies. Health information management practices, such as documentation quality and privacy safeguards, are frequently assessed as part of the accreditation process.
  131. The correct answer is D (Accreditation Canada, which conducts voluntary external reviews against national quality standards.): Accreditation Canada conducts on-site and remote surveys evaluating a health organization's compliance with national quality and safety standards, and health information management functions such as record completeness, coding accuracy, and privacy practices are typically part of what surveyors assess. Preparing documentation evidence in advance of such a review is a common HIM department responsibility. CIHI, by contrast, is focused on data collection and reporting rather than conducting organizational quality surveys.
  132. The correct answer is A (The Canadian Institute for Health Information, which standardizes and reports national health data.): CIHI maintains the Discharge Abstract Database, which collects standardized demographic, administrative, and clinical data on hospital inpatient discharges from most Canadian provinces and territories, supporting national reporting, case-mix grouping, and health system analysis. Health information management professionals are directly responsible for abstracting and submitting this data accurately. This database is one of CIHI's most significant data holdings relevant to CHIM practice.
  133. The correct answer is B (Health Canada handles broader regulatory policy, while PHAC focuses on public health surveillance and emergencies.): although both are federal health bodies, Health Canada holds broader responsibilities including administering the Canada Health Act and regulating drugs, medical devices, and consumer products, while the Public Health Agency of Canada was created as a distinct agency to concentrate specifically on public health surveillance, infectious disease control, and emergency preparedness. This split reflects lessons learned from earlier public health emergency responses. Correctly separating these mandates is frequently tested.
  134. The correct answer is A (Describe her general skills and experience while declining to share her current employer's specific internal practices.): Professional integrity requires respecting confidentiality obligations to a current employer even while pursuing new opportunities, which means she can describe her general skills and experience without disclosing specific proprietary internal practices. Sharing internal details to strengthen a candidacy, even with a confidentiality request to the interviewer, still constitutes an improper disclosure. Declining to discuss her job duties at all is an overcorrection when general experience can reasonably be described without breaching confidentiality.
  135. The correct answer is B (Query the physician to clarify whether the complication should be included in the final coded diagnoses.): A genuine discrepancy between documentation within the chart and the physician's final summary regarding a clinically significant complication should be resolved through a formal physician query, since coders cannot independently determine which source should govern the final code assignment. Automatically including or excluding the complication based on one source alone risks either overcoding or undercoding the encounter. Asking the patient directly is inappropriate since coding must be based on the clinician's own documentation, not the coder gathering new clinical history.
  136. The correct answer is D (The duty of confidentiality.): HIM professionals must protect personal health information regardless of a patient's public status, and casual disclosure in a cafeteria has no legitimate business purpose. Confidentiality applies equally to public figures and private patients, and identifying details combined with a diagnosis meet the threshold for a breach. Competence and documentation issues are not what occurred here since the core failure was an unauthorized disclosure.
  137. The correct answer is A (Using clinical information to identify potential candidates without prior patient consent raises a privacy concern.): Using health record information to identify and screen potential fundraising candidates before any patient consent has been obtained uses protected clinical information for a purpose the patient never authorized, regardless of how worthwhile the fundraising cause may be. The tone of later contact does not address the underlying unauthorized use of clinical data during candidate selection. A privacy compliant approach would typically involve opt-in processes where patients themselves choose to participate rather than being pre-selected from clinical records.
  138. The correct answer is C (Investigate objectively using only the information genuinely necessary to identify the root cause of the error.): A fair, objective root cause investigation should use only the information genuinely necessary to determine what happened, balancing thoroughness against unnecessary intrusion into staff performance details not relevant to the specific error. Avoiding the investigation entirely fails to resolve a legitimate data accuracy concern. Assigning fault based on department history or who raised the dispute first, rather than actual evidence, would be an unfair and inaccurate resolution.
  139. The correct answer is D (Establish clear expectations and training requiring coders to independently verify every suggested code against documentation.): Automated coding suggestions can be a useful tool, but coding integrity requires that coders continue to independently verify suggested codes against actual documentation rather than accepting them uncritically, so clear expectations and training should accompany any deployment. Assuming the technology is simply more accurate ignores the real risk of over-reliance regardless of overall system performance. A permanent avoidance of the technology, or limiting it only to experienced coders, does not address the underlying need for consistent verification practices across all users.
  140. The correct answer is B (Confirm the colleague has a genuine care relationship with the patient before sharing treatment history.): Sharing patient information, even among clinical colleagues, must be tied to a genuine current need to know related to actual patient care, so confirming the colleague's specific care relationship before sharing anything is the appropriate first step regardless of good intentions. Sharing immediately based on intention alone, even in a limited summary form, still risks an unauthorized disclosure if no genuine care relationship exists. Declining without any explanation misses an opportunity to educate the colleague about the correct process to follow instead.
  141. The correct answer is C (Address the pattern directly since omitting legitimately documented diagnoses compromises data completeness and accuracy.): Coding integrity requires capturing all clinically relevant, properly documented diagnoses, not only the principal diagnosis, because secondary diagnoses affect case complexity data, research, and appropriate resource allocation. Tolerating shortcuts because the principal diagnosis remains technically accurate ignores the broader importance of complete coding. Waiting for an external audit to catch a known internal pattern is a reactive rather than proactive response to a known accuracy issue.
  142. The correct answer is A (Recommend a documented, consistent process for verifying identity and tracking every release, even on paper.): Even a paper based environment requires a documented, consistent process for verifying requester identity and tracking releases, since the absence of technology does not remove the underlying ethical and legal obligations around confidentiality and accountable disclosure. Handling requests informally without any written procedure risks inconsistent, unverifiable practice. Delaying legitimate patient access requests indefinitely until a future system upgrade fails the patient's genuine and timely right of access.
  143. The correct answer is C (Accept only if she receives adequate orientation and ensures the work is properly supervised and reviewed.): Professional accountability allows taking on new responsibilities during genuine need, provided the individual secures the training and oversight necessary to perform the work competently and safely. Assuming universal competence across all records tasks ignores real differences in required knowledge. An outright refusal is not necessary if appropriate support is arranged, and keeping the arrangement undocumented undermines accountability.
  144. The correct answer is A (Document the incident, assess actual risk, and take corrective action regardless of the low apparent impact.): Even a temporary, seemingly low impact incident like misplaced records during a move should be properly documented and risk assessed, with appropriate corrective action taken to prevent recurrence, rather than dismissed simply because the records were eventually recovered. Treating it as equivalent to a confirmed unauthorized disclosure overstates the actual known risk based on available evidence. Waiting for a patient inquiry before addressing a known internal control gap is a reactive rather than proactive approach to record security.
  145. The correct answer is B (Department level tracking should only be included if it does not risk identifying individual respondents.): Even an internal staff survey should consider whether granular tracking, such as by department, could realistically allow identification of individual respondents in a small department, undermining the anonymity the survey design intends to provide. Assuming tracking is always beneficial ignores this identifiability risk in smaller groups. Treating staff survey anonymity as unimportant compared to patient information ignores that staff also have legitimate expectations of confidentiality in an anonymous survey context.
  146. The correct answer is D (Volunteers accessing patient contact information should meet screening and training standards proportionate to that access.): Any individual accessing patient information, including volunteers, should meet screening and training standards proportionate to the sensitivity of that access, since patient contact information still carries privacy protection regardless of whether the person accessing it is paid staff or an unpaid volunteer. An absolute prohibition on any volunteer role is overly broad when appropriate screening can address the actual risk. Simply asking for care without formal screening or training does not provide a meaningful safeguard.
  147. The correct answer is C (Explain that these themes form a professional obligation tied to trust, accuracy, and patient welfare.): While themes like confidentiality, competence, and integrity may resemble general good workplace conduct, in HIM practice they carry specific professional weight because inaccurate or improperly disclosed health information can directly affect patient care, funding, research, and legal outcomes. Treating them as generic habits understates their direct connection to patient welfare and public trust in the profession. These obligations apply to every certified HIM professional regardless of position or seniority level.
  148. The correct answer is B (Raise the concern that skipping verification compromises accuracy and seek another way to meet the deadline.): A supervisor's instruction that would compromise coding accuracy, such as skipping documentation verification, should be raised directly rather than simply followed, since coding integrity cannot be sacrificed for scheduling convenience regardless of who issues the instruction. Selectively skipping verification only for simple charts still introduces the same underlying accuracy risk. Ignoring a legitimate deadline concern entirely without addressing it collaboratively does not resolve the operational pressure the supervisor is facing.
  149. The correct answer is D (Support timely, honest disclosure to affected patients and appropriate authorities as required by policy and law.): Ethical breach response requires timely and honest communication with those affected and with regulatory authorities as required, because patients need accurate information to protect themselves and organizations have both legal and ethical transparency obligations. Concealing the breach or waiting for a patient to ask both prioritize reputation over patient interests. Reasonable timelines tied to investigation needs are appropriate, but indefinite delay is not.
  150. The correct answer is A (Health information practices, technology, and standards evolve, and ongoing learning is needed to stay current.): Coding classification systems, privacy legislation, technology platforms, and health information standards change over time, so competence based solely on original training gradually becomes outdated even if day to day performance still appears adequate. Framing continuing education as a formality or a revenue source misunderstands its purpose of protecting patients and data quality. The obligation applies to every certified professional, not only those seeking career advancement.
  151. The correct answer is B (Treat the access as a confidentiality breach and follow the organization's disciplinary and reporting procedures.): Intent does not change the fact that accessing a record without an authorized purpose is an unauthorized disclosure risk and a serious ethical and often legal violation. Consistent application of policy protects patient trust and demonstrates organizational accountability for privacy. Informal reminders or narrow restrictions understate the seriousness of repeated unauthorized access and fail to address the underlying breach.
  152. The correct answer is D (Recorded telehealth visits containing clinical content should receive the same protections as other health record documentation.): Recorded telehealth visits that contain clinical content are a form of health record documentation and should generally receive equivalent retention, security, and access protections as other parts of the record, since the format itself does not diminish the sensitivity of the clinical information captured. Treating video content more casually ignores this equivalence. Immediate deletion could compromise both patient care continuity and legal record requirements depending on applicable retention rules.
  153. The correct answer is C (Present an honest, balanced assessment reflecting both strengths and reservations rather than a one sided endorsement.): Professional integrity requires that public professional commentary remain honest and balanced, disclosing genuine reservations alongside positive experience, even when a paid engagement creates pressure toward a one sided endorsement. A completely one sided presentation misleads the audience about the product's actual limitations. A paid speaking opportunity is not inherently unethical provided the content remains honest, so an outright refusal is not the necessary response.
  154. The correct answer is A (Decline the request and explain that accessing records without a legitimate work purpose violates confidentiality obligations.): Accessing any personal health information requires a legitimate, job-related need to know, and curiosity from a friend never meets that standard. Even a single data element such as an admission date is protected information and unauthorized access can constitute a privacy breach and grounds for discipline. Redirecting elsewhere is reasonable but does not address the ethical duty to refuse the request outright and explain why.

Practice Test 5 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is C (Glucagon, a hormone secreted by pancreatic α cells to raise blood glucose.): glucagon raises blood glucose by stimulating glycogen breakdown and glucose release from the liver, directly opposing the glucose-lowering action of insulin. Option B describes insulin itself, the hormone this question asks about the opposite of. Option A describes a hormone that generally inhibits both insulin and glucagon rather than directly raising glucose. Option D describes a gastrointestinal hormone unrelated to glucose counter-regulation.
  2. The correct answer is A (Long bones, such as the femur and humerus, with a shaft longer than it is wide.): long bones such as the femur, humerus, and tibia have a shaft longer than they are wide and typically function in weight-bearing and leverage. Option C describes bones such as carpals that are roughly cube shaped, unlike the femur. Option B describes bones such as the sternum or skull bones, structurally different from a long bone. Option D describes bones with complex shapes such as vertebrae, not the femur.
  3. The correct answer is D (Articular cartilage covering the ends of the bones.): osteoarthritis involves progressive wear and breakdown of the smooth articular cartilage, leading to bone-on-bone friction, pain, and stiffness. Option B describes the synovial membrane, more directly implicated in rheumatoid arthritis rather than osteoarthritis. Option C describes tendons, which are not the primary tissue degenerated in osteoarthritis. Option A describes the periosteum, an outer bone covering unrelated to joint cartilage.
  4. The correct answer is B (Surgical fusion of a joint to eliminate motion permanently.): arthrodesis is a procedure that fuses two bones across a joint space, eliminating movement to reduce pain from instability or severe arthritis. Option A describes arthroplasty, a different orthopedic procedure. Option C describes external fixation, an unrelated fracture management technique. Option D describes a debridement procedure that does not include fusion.
  5. The correct answer is A (Skeletal muscle tissue, the voluntary muscle type damaged in a crush injury.): rhabdomyolysis describes the breakdown of damaged skeletal muscle fibers, releasing myoglobin and other intracellular contents that can precipitate acute kidney injury when filtered by the kidneys. Option B describes fat tissue, not the tissue destroyed in this condition. Option C describes cartilage, unrelated to rhabdomyolysis. Option D describes bone tissue, also unrelated to this particular muscle breakdown process.
  6. The correct answer is D (A tendon connects muscle to bone, while a ligament connects bone to bone.): tendons transmit the force of muscle contraction to bone, while ligaments stabilize joints by connecting adjacent bones directly. Option A reverses these two definitions incorrectly. Option C misdescribes both structures as connecting muscle to muscle, which neither does. Option B misdescribes both structures with an incorrect bone-to-cartilage connection.
  7. The correct answer is C (Infection and inflammation of the bone and bone marrow.): osteo refers to bone and myel refers to marrow, with the suffix itis denoting inflammation, together describing an infectious process within the bone itself, often introduced through an open fracture. Option A describes a benign cyst, an unrelated noninfectious lesion. Option B describes a stress fracture, a mechanical rather than infectious process. Option D describes osteoporosis, a distinct degenerative condition.
  8. The correct answer is A (Shortening and stiffening of muscles, tendons, or connective tissue.): prolonged immobilization allows soft tissue fibers to shorten and lose elasticity, permanently limiting the joint's normal range of motion. Option B describes the opposite change, excessive laxity rather than shortening. Option C describes an acute inflammatory process, not the chronic shortening seen in a contracture. Option D describes a traumatic rupture, an unrelated acute injury rather than a chronic adaptive change.
  9. The correct answer is D (The sarcoplasmic reticulum, a specialized form of endoplasmic reticulum.): this organelle stores calcium ions within the muscle fiber and releases them into the cytoplasm to trigger the sliding filament mechanism of contraction. Option A describes the organelle generating ATP for contraction, not the calcium storage site. Option C describes the cell's genetic control center, unrelated to calcium storage. Option B describes the outer muscle fiber membrane, which propagates the electrical signal but does not itself store calcium.
  10. The correct answer is B (The synovial membrane, which becomes chronically inflamed as the immune system attacks the joint lining.): rheumatoid arthritis is an autoimmune condition in which the immune system attacks the synovial lining of joints, causing chronic inflammation that can eventually destroy cartilage and bone. Option A describes the primary process in osteoarthritis, a mechanically driven condition rather than autoimmune. Option C describes a hematologic malignancy process, unrelated to rheumatoid arthritis. Option D describes a peripheral nerve condition, not a joint-specific autoimmune process.
  11. The correct answer is A (The bone is broken into three or more separate fragments.): comminuted describes a fracture pattern in which the bone shatters into multiple pieces rather than breaking cleanly into two segments. Option B describes a closed fracture, a separate classification concerning the skin. Option C describes an oblique fracture, a different pattern classification. Option D describes a greenstick fracture, typically seen in pediatric bone that is more pliable.
  12. The correct answer is C (The epidermis, the outermost layer of the skin providing the primary barrier function.): this outermost layer consists of stratified squamous epithelium and provides the primary physical barrier against pathogens, water loss, and environmental damage. Option A describes the layer beneath the epidermis containing blood vessels and nerves, not the outermost layer. Option B describes the deepest layer, composed largely of fat, not the outer barrier. Option D describes connective tissue beneath the skin, not the outer epidermal barrier.
  13. The correct answer is D (The epidermis and a portion of the underlying dermis.): second-degree, or partial-thickness, burns damage the epidermis and extend into the dermis, typically producing blistering and significant pain. Option A describes a first-degree burn, a more superficial injury. Option C describes a third-degree, or full-thickness, burn extending beyond the dermis. Option B describes a fourth-degree burn, an even deeper injury than second-degree.
  14. The correct answer is B (A bacterial infection spreading through the dermis and subcutaneous tissue.): cellulitis is a bacterial skin infection that spreads through deeper skin layers, producing the redness, warmth, and swelling described in this scenario. Option A describes a nevus or similar melanocytic lesion, an unrelated benign process. Option C describes a condition such as bullous pemphigoid, a different pathological mechanism. Option D describes a superficial fungal infection, which does not typically extend into deeper tissue as cellulitis does.
  15. The correct answer is A (The proliferative stage, during which new blood vessels and collagen rebuild the wound.): during this stage, fibroblasts deposit new collagen and new blood vessels form through angiogenesis to rebuild the structural framework of the healing tissue. Option C describes the initial clotting stage that stops bleeding, not tissue rebuilding. Option B describes the stage dominated by immune cell activity clearing debris, occurring before proliferation. Option D describes the final remodeling stage, which strengthens tissue after the proliferative stage is largely complete.
  16. The correct answer is C (Melanocytes, the pigment-producing cells within the epidermis from which melanoma arises.): melanoma is a malignancy arising from melanocytes, the cells responsible for producing melanin pigment that gives skin its colour. Option A describes the predominant epidermal cell type, but keratinocyte malignancies produce basal or squamous cell carcinoma rather than melanoma. Option B describes immune cells within the skin, unrelated to melanoma's cell of origin. Option D describes dermal connective tissue cells, not the pigment-producing cells implicated in melanoma.
  17. The correct answer is D (Sustained pressure impairing local blood flow and causing tissue ischemia.): pressure ulcers develop when prolonged compression, typically over a bony prominence, restricts capillary blood flow long enough to cause tissue death. Option A describes a mechanism relevant to blisters or calluses, not the primary cause of pressure ulcers. Option C describes an unrelated dermatologic reaction. Option B describes a viral process such as shingles, unrelated to pressure-induced ulceration.
  18. The correct answer is B (The seminiferous tubules within the testes, the actual site of sperm cell production.): sperm cells are produced through spermatogenesis occurring within these tightly coiled tubules located inside each testis. Option A describes an accessory gland contributing fluid to semen, not the site of sperm production. Option C describes the structure where sperm mature and are stored after production, not where they are made. Option D describes another accessory gland contributing seminal fluid, not sperm production itself.
  19. The correct answer is B (Correct the affected abstracts and resubmit them to CIHI to maintain data integrity for national reporting.): When a systemic error, such as an incorrect institution number caused by a configuration issue, is identified after submission, the appropriate corrective action is to fix the affected abstracts and resubmit the corrected records to CIHI so national data remains accurate and reliable for funding and reporting purposes. Option A downplays the importance of accurate facility identification, which is essential to correctly attributing data. Option C inappropriately shifts responsibility away from the submitting hospital, and option D is inappropriate since deleting records without resubmitting corrected data would leave a gap in national reporting rather than resolving the error.
  20. The correct answer is C (It distinguishes how the patient entered hospital care, used in case-mix analysis and health system planning.): The entry code data element captures how a patient entered hospital care, for example through an elective versus urgent or emergency admission, information that is used in case-mix analysis, resource planning, and comparative health system reporting across facilities. Option A incorrectly narrows this element to mode of transportation, which is unrelated. Option B wrongly limits its purpose to unit assignment logistics, an operational matter separate from CIHI's standardized abstracting fields, and option D is false since entry code remains an active, required data element on the DAD abstract.
  21. The correct answer is B (Under CIHI's same-day readmission reporting rules, consistent with ICD-10-CA conventions.): CIHI provides specific reporting guidance for readmissions occurring within a short window, such as 24 hours, of a previous discharge for a related condition, and these situations are handled according to defined same-day readmission rules that are applied consistently with ICD-10-CA abstracting conventions rather than left to individual coder discretion. Option A oversimplifies the situation by ignoring that CIHI has specific rules governing how closely related, closely timed readmissions are reported. Option C is incorrect because both encounters are legitimate episodes of care that must be reported, and option D misapplies the concept of MRDx, which is determined independently for each admission based on that stay's own documentation.
  22. The correct answer is C (A most responsible diagnosis that does not match documentation supporting the greatest resource use.): A coding quality auditor reviewing DAD submissions specifically looks for substantive coding errors, such as an MRDx that is not actually supported by documentation showing it accounted for the greatest resource use during the stay, since this directly affects case-mix grouping accuracy and funding. Options A, B, and D all describe correctly formatted or consistent data elements, an appropriately formatted date, a matching institution number, and a documented discharge disposition, which are examples of what an audit would confirm as correct rather than flag as an error.
  23. The correct answer is B (DAD data also supports health system planning, quality indicators, and national comparative reporting by CIHI.): Beyond its direct role in CMG+ funding calculations, timely and complete DAD abstracting supports broader uses of the data, including health system capacity planning, calculation of national quality and safety indicators, and comparative reporting across provinces and facilities, all of which depend on accurate and current information. Option A drastically understates the DAD's national significance beyond a single hospital's own use. Option C is false since abstracting quality still depends on trained coders reviewing documentation, not on automatic algorithmic correction, and option D incorrectly limits DAD data's audience to private insurers when its primary use is public health system management.
  24. The correct answer is C (Initiate a physician query to clarify the timing and clinical significance of the condition.): When documentation is genuinely ambiguous about whether a condition was present on admission or developed during the stay, the recommended and defensible approach is to initiate a physician query so the timing and clinical significance can be clarified directly with the responsible provider before the diagnosis is coded and typed. Option A risks introducing an inaccurate assumption into the abstract without confirmation. Option B is inappropriate since omitting a clinically relevant condition rather than clarifying it undermines data completeness, and option D incorrectly defaults an ambiguous condition to MRDx status without any basis for that determination.
  25. The correct answer is A (Emergency department visits and other ambulatory care encounters such as day surgery and clinic visits.): The National Ambulatory Care Reporting System is designed to capture ambulatory care activity, including emergency department visits, day surgery, and outpatient clinic encounters, distinguishing it from the DAD's focus on inpatient admissions. This scope allows CIHI to monitor emergency and outpatient system performance separately from inpatient care. Option B incorrectly describes long-term care admissions, which fall outside NACRS's ambulatory care scope. Option C wrongly limits NACRS to inpatient surgery, and option D incorrectly describes home care visits, which are not part of NACRS's defined ambulatory encounter types.
  26. The correct answer is A (The National Ambulatory Care Reporting System, since the visit did not result in admission.): Because the patient was assessed, treated, and discharged home the same day without being admitted as an inpatient, this encounter falls within NACRS's scope as an ambulatory emergency department visit rather than an inpatient stay captured in the DAD. Option B is incorrect because the DAD is specifically reserved for inpatient discharges, not routine emergency department visits that do not result in admission. Option C is false since NACRS was created specifically to capture emergency department encounters, and option D understates NACRS's role as the established national database for this type of visit, not merely a provincial billing tool.
  27. The correct answer is C (ICD-10-CA, the same diagnosis classification system used for inpatient discharge abstracting.): NACRS diagnoses are coded using ICD-10-CA, the identical classification system used for inpatient DAD abstracting, ensuring consistency in diagnosis terminology and comparability of data across the inpatient and ambulatory care settings in Canada's national health information system. Option A incorrectly suggests a separate proprietary system exists for emergency coding. Option B is wrong because CPT is a United States procedural coding system, not a Canadian diagnosis classification, and option D incorrectly claims SNOMED CT is the required classification for NACRS diagnoses, when ICD-10-CA is actually the standard used.
  28. The correct answer is A (The emergency visit portion is captured in NACRS while the inpatient stay is captured separately.): When a patient is triaged, assessed, and then admitted directly from the emergency department, the emergency department encounter portion is recorded in NACRS while the subsequent inpatient stay is captured as its own separate DAD abstract, reflecting the distinct scope and purpose of each database. Option B incorrectly combines both phases of care into a single NACRS record, which does not reflect how CIHI's databases are structured. Option C is false since both portions of this encounter are captured across the two databases, and option D incorrectly omits the NACRS portion entirely.
  29. This directly affects funding models built on CMG+ output, since hospitals are compensated based on the weighted case mix their coded data reflects.   44. Strengthening physician documentation practices and the coder query process helps ensure conditions that affect resource use are actually captured as type 1 or type 2 diagnoses on the abstract. Targeted education addresses specific gaps identified in the audit, such as missed complications or under-specified diagnoses, so future abstracts better reflect true acuity. Reducing staff or halting DAD submission would only worsen data quality and do nothing to correct the underlying documentation and coding gap.
  45. The correct answer is D (Each case mix group has an associated expected length of stay used as a resource planning benchmark.): Every CMG+ assignment carries a published expected length of stay, or ELOS, derived from national utilization data for similarly grouped cases, which hospitals use to benchmark actual stays and plan bed resources. A stay that significantly exceeds its ELOS may trigger an outlier adjustment in funding calculations, reflecting the additional resources consumed. This makes ELOS a direct output of the CMG+ methodology rather than something calculated independently or tied to geography. Type 2 diagnoses specifically capture conditions that develop during the episode of care and materially affect resource use, which is exactly what a transfer to intensive care represents. Including this diagnosis allows the CMG+ grouping to reflect the true complexity and extended length of stay the case actually generated. Leaving the event off the abstract, or removing the original elective procedure code, would misrepresent the episode and understate the resources consumed.
  47. This reflects the reality that newborn care needs are driven heavily by physiologic maturity rather than a discrete acute condition. Adult and pediatric CMG+ groups, by contrast, are built primarily from the coded most responsible diagnosis, comorbidities, and interventions. Since CMG+ resource intensity weights directly influence hospital funding, HIM leadership has a strong incentive to confirm accuracy rather than assume it, particularly where coded complexity appears to diverge from clinical reality. Findings from such a study typically feed into targeted coder education or documentation improvement initiatives.   49. The correct answer is C (Auto-populated diagnoses may not reflect diagnosis typing or clinical significance, risking miscoded case mix groups.): When an EHR auto-populates diagnosis fields from a problem list without coder review, there is a real risk that chronic or resolved conditions get carried forward as if clinically significant to the current admission, or that the diagnosis typing needed to distinguish pre-admit comorbidities from new complications is lost entirely. Coders rely on clinical judgment and documentation review to determine whether a condition meets CIHI's criteria for reportability and correct typing, something an automated field population cannot replicate. This risk directly threatens CMG+ accuracy because incorrect typing changes which diagnoses count toward resource intensity weight.
